I’ve definitely felt down, but I don’t know how to measure it that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m trying to think of something. My wife keeps trying with our old Sunday routine, you know, coffee, maybe a drive, little errands—there’s this one place she likes that has these ridiculous muffins, and I used to joke they were half the reason I went.\n\nBut now I’m there and I know it should feel nice. I know I should feel close to her in those moments. Same with work—if something goes well, everybody’s relieved, and I can tell it’s a good thing. I just don’t really feel the happiness from it. It’s more like… I register it, but it doesn’t get inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a two-week period since the accident where you felt consistently depressed or low?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I know that sounds like dodging it, but I don’t track time that way very well anymore. There are stretches where I’m low, yeah… where everything feels kind of gray. But whether it was exactly two weeks, consistently, I don’t know.\n\nMy wife might say yes. She notices patterns more than I do. I’ll be thinking about, you know, some stupid work thing or whether I remembered to move the trash cans, and then I realize a bunch of days have gone by and I’ve been the same. But I can’t honestly give you a clean two-week answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently depressed or low for any two-week period since the accident?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a straight yes or no, but I honestly don’t know. Time’s gotten kind of slippery for me since then. Like, I can tell you I’ve had bad stretches—low, flat, not really myself—but if you’re asking me to mark out fourteen days on a calendar, I can’t.\n\nMy wife notices that kind of thing more. She’ll say I’ve been “off” for a while, and I’ll be like… was that a week? Was that longer? I don’t know. I get wrapped up in work stuff or little things around the house, and then it’s like the days just sort of stack up without me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure or enjoyment in?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can go through the motions of things that used to be nice. My wife still tries to get us to do our Sunday stuff—coffee, a drive, stopping somewhere—and I know it’s supposed to feel good. I can see her trying, and part of me is thinking I should feel close to her, I should enjoy this.\n\nBut it’s like there’s a pane of glass there or something. Same at work—if a project goes right or people are happy, I understand that it’s good news. I might even say the right thing. But I don’t really feel the happiness from it. It just doesn’t land the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s less like I suddenly hate those things and more like they don’t reach me anymore.\n\nMy wife tries to do our old Sunday routine—coffee, driving around, stopping someplace we used to like—and I’m there, physically, and I know it should feel good. I know I should feel close to her. But it’s just… flat. Like I’m watching us do it instead of being in it.\n\nSame thing at work. If something goes well, I can recognize that it went well, I can say, “good job,” all that manager stuff. But I don’t feel the lift from it. That’s honestly part of what brings me in—there’s not much real happiness or closeness anymore, even when the situation is supposed to have it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where you felt significantly depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly. Like, I know there have been times I’ve been really down, but “distinct periods” makes it sound like there was a clear start and stop, and I don’t really track it that way.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I’ve been dragging for a while, but then I get distracted by work or something at home—like the garage still needs fixing, and I keep telling myself I’ll handle it—and then I lose track of how long I’ve felt that way. So… I don’t know. I can’t point to a specific stretch and say, “that was the depressed period.”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been keeping track of it like that.\n\nSome days I eat because it’s there, or because my wife asks if I’ve eaten, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s more or less than before. Workdays get weird too—meetings run into lunch, someone brings food into the break room, then I’m thinking about whether I answered an email wrong or if I forgot to call somebody back… and then the whole day’s gone.\n\nSo, appetite specifically? I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my clothes still basically fit, so I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic.\n\nBut I haven’t weighed myself. We have a scale somewhere—my wife moved it when she cleaned out the bathroom closet, and then that turned into this whole thing with old towels and boxes from when we renovated… sorry. I guess what I’m saying is I haven’t noticed a clear weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep pattern since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been pretty bad, I guess. Most nights I’ll lie there and my brain just goes right back to the plant, like I’m replaying it whether I want to or not.\n\nAnd then if I do fall asleep, it’s not like good sleep. I’ll jolt awake from dreams about the line, or I’ll wake up at like 3 or 4 in the morning and just… feel like I didn’t sleep at all. Then I’m up thinking about stupid things too, like whether I left something on my desk or if I need to check the schedule, but really it always kind of circles back to that place.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself since the accident?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly. I haven’t really thought of it in those exact words.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life since the accident?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I don’t think of it like that exactly. “Ending my life” sounds so final, and I don’t sit down and, you know, make decisions about something like that.\n\nSometimes I just get tired of everything feeling so loud in my head, and then I end up thinking about completely other things, like bills or whether I replied to an email, or my son—well, he had this school thing last month and I almost missed it because I got stuck at work, and then I felt terrible about that too.\n\nSo… I don’t know. Two weeks is hard to pin down.\n\nMy wife would probably have a better answer—she tracks things more than I do. She’ll say, “You’ve been like this for days,” and I’m thinking about work schedules or whether the garage door needs fixing, and then suddenly it’s the next week. So… I’m not sure. I’ve definitely felt down, but I don’t know how to measure it that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m trying to think of something. My wife keeps trying with our old Sunday routine, you know, coffee, maybe a drive, little errands—there’s this one place she likes that has these ridiculous muffins, and I used to joke they were half the reason I went.\n\nBut now I’m there and I know it should feel nice. I know I should feel close to her in those moments. Same with work—if something goes well, everybody’s relieved, and I can tell it’s a good thing. I just don’t really feel the happiness from it. It’s more like… I register it, but it doesn’t get inside.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a two-week period since the accident where you felt consistently depressed or low?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I know that sounds like dodging it, but I don’t track time that way very well anymore. There are stretches where I’m low, yeah… where everything feels kind of gray. But whether it was exactly two weeks, consistently, I don’t know.\n\nMy wife might say yes. She notices patterns more than I do. I’ll be thinking about, you know, some stupid work thing or whether I remembered to move the trash cans, and then I realize a bunch of days have gone by and I’ve been the same. But I can’t honestly give you a clean two-week answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently depressed or low for any two-week period since the accident?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a straight yes or no, but I honestly don’t know. Time’s gotten kind of slippery for me since then. Like, I can tell you I’ve had bad stretches—low, flat, not really myself—but if you’re asking me to mark out fourteen days on a calendar, I can’t.\n\nMy wife notices that kind of thing more. She’ll say I’ve been “off” for a while, and I’ll be like… was that a week? Was that longer? I don’t know. I get wrapped up in work stuff or little things around the house, and then it’s like the days just sort of stack up without me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure or enjoyment in?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can go through the motions of things that used to be nice. My wife still tries to get us to do our Sunday stuff—coffee, a drive, stopping somewhere—and I know it’s supposed to feel good. I can see her trying, and part of me is thinking I should feel close to her, I should enjoy this.\n\nBut it’s like there’s a pane of glass there or something. Same at work—if a project goes right or people are happy, I understand that it’s good news. I might even say the right thing. But I don’t really feel the happiness from it. It just doesn’t land the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s less like I suddenly hate those things and more like they don’t reach me anymore.\n\nMy wife tries to do our old Sunday routine—coffee, driving around, stopping someplace we used to like—and I’m there, physically, and I know it should feel good. I know I should feel close to her. But it’s just… flat. Like I’m watching us do it instead of being in it.\n\nSame thing at work. If something goes well, I can recognize that it went well, I can say, “good job,” all that manager stuff. But I don’t feel the lift from it. That’s honestly part of what brings me in—there’s not much real happiness or closeness anymore, even when the situation is supposed to have it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where you felt significantly depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly. Like, I know there have been times I’ve been really down, but “distinct periods” makes it sound like there was a clear start and stop, and I don’t really track it that way.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I’ve been dragging for a while, but then I get distracted by work or something at home—like the garage still needs fixing, and I keep telling myself I’ll handle it—and then I lose track of how long I’ve felt that way. So… I don’t know. I can’t point to a specific stretch and say, “that was the depressed period.”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been keeping track of it like that.\n\nSome days I eat because it’s there, or because my wife asks if I’ve eaten, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s more or less than before. Workdays get weird too—meetings run into lunch, someone brings food into the break room, then I’m thinking about whether I answered an email wrong or if I forgot to call somebody back… and then the whole day’s gone.\n\nSo, appetite specifically? I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my clothes still basically fit, so I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic.\n\nBut I haven’t weighed myself. We have a scale somewhere—my wife moved it when she cleaned out the bathroom closet, and then that turned into this whole thing with old towels and boxes from when we renovated… sorry. I guess what I’m saying is I haven’t noticed a clear weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep pattern since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been pretty bad, I guess. Most nights I’ll lie there and my brain just goes right back to the plant, like I’m replaying it whether I want to or not.\n\nAnd then if I do fall asleep, it’s not like good sleep. I’ll jolt awake from dreams about the line, or I’ll wake up at like 3 or 4 in the morning and just… feel like I didn’t sleep at all. Then I’m up thinking about stupid things too, like whether I left something on my desk or if I need to check the schedule, but really it always kind of circles back to that place.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself since the accident?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, “hurting myself” sounds very direct, and I don’t sit there making some kind of plan, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I get stuck in my head and everything feels like too much, and then I’ll think, you know, I just wish I could shut it off for a while. But then I start thinking about my wife needing the car serviced, or some thing at work that I’m supposed to handle, and it kind of turns into a whole list of stuff. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it in those exact words.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life since the accident?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I don’t think of it like that exactly. “Ending my life” sounds so final, and I don’t sit down and, you know, make decisions about something like that.\n\nSometimes I just get tired of everything feeling so loud in my head, and then I end up thinking about completely other things, like bills or whether I replied to an email, or my son—well, he had this school thing last month and I almost missed it because I got stuck at work, and then I felt terrible about that too.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I guess I have a hard time separating what’s just wanting everything to stop for a minute from what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die since the accident?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, wanting to die is a big way to put it, and I don’t usually say it to myself like that.\n\nSometimes I just feel worn out, like I don’t want to deal with another day of everything being like this, but then my mind jumps to something else, like whether I paid the mortgage or if someone at work is waiting on me for some report. And then I’m thinking about how the garage door’s been making that noise again, which sounds stupid, but it’s just… my head goes everywhere.\n\nSo I’m not sure how to answer that straight. I don’t know if I’d call it wanting to die.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of not wanting to live anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. Not wanting to live anymore sounds like… like I’ve reached some clear conclusion, and I don’t think that’s what happens in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I get overwhelmed and then I can’t tell if I mean I don’t want life, or I just don’t want the next phone call, the next errand, the next—like yesterday I spent twenty minutes looking for my keys and then realized they were in my coat pocket, and somehow that felt like proof I can’t keep anything straight. Which sounds ridiculous when I say it.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I don’t think I can give you a clean yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt really low, yeah, but “most of the day, nearly every day” is hard for me to pin down.\n\nSome days I wake up already feeling heavy, and then other days I get distracted by work stuff or something at home, and I’ll realize I haven’t really checked in with myself at all. Like last week I spent half the day dealing with some scheduling mess at work, and by the time I got home I couldn’t even tell if I was depressed or just exhausted.\n\nSo… I’m not saying no, exactly. I just don’t know how to measure it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt depressed for most of the day, for at least two weeks straight, at any point since the accident?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. I don’t… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.

I’ll be thinking about, you know, some stupid work thing or whether I remembered to move the trash cans, and then I realize a bunch of days have gone by and I’ve been the same. But I can’t honestly give you a clean two-week answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently depressed or low for any two-week period since the accident?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a straight yes or no, but I honestly don’t know. Time’s gotten kind of slippery for me since then. Like, I can tell you I’ve had bad stretches—low, flat, not really myself—but if you’re asking me to mark out fourteen days on a calendar, I can’t.\n\nMy wife notices that kind of thing more. She’ll say I’ve been “off” for a while, and I’ll be like… was that a week? Was that longer? I don’t know. I get wrapped up in work stuff or little things around the house, and then it’s like the days just sort of stack up without me paying attention.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure or enjoyment in?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I can go through the motions of things that used to be nice. My wife still tries to get us to do our Sunday stuff—coffee, a drive, stopping somewhere—and I know it’s supposed to feel good. I can see her trying, and part of me is thinking I should feel close to her, I should enjoy this.\n\nBut it’s like there’s a pane of glass there or something. Same at work—if a project goes right or people are happy, I understand that it’s good news. I might even say the right thing. But I don’t really feel the happiness from it. It just doesn’t land the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s less like I suddenly hate those things and more like they don’t reach me anymore.\n\nMy wife tries to do our old Sunday routine—coffee, driving around, stopping someplace we used to like—and I’m there, physically, and I know it should feel good. I know I should feel close to her. But it’s just… flat. Like I’m watching us do it instead of being in it.\n\nSame thing at work. If something goes well, I can recognize that it went well, I can say, “good job,” all that manager stuff. But I don’t feel the lift from it. That’s honestly part of what brings me in—there’s not much real happiness or closeness anymore, even when the situation is supposed to have it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where you felt significantly depressed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate it that cleanly. Like, I know there have been times I’ve been really down, but “distinct periods” makes it sound like there was a clear start and stop, and I don’t really track it that way.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I’ve been dragging for a while, but then I get distracted by work or something at home—like the garage still needs fixing, and I keep telling myself I’ll handle it—and then I lose track of how long I’ve felt that way. So… I don’t know. I can’t point to a specific stretch and say, “that was the depressed period.”\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been keeping track of it like that.\n\nSome days I eat because it’s there, or because my wife asks if I’ve eaten, but I couldn’t tell you if it’s more or less than before. Workdays get weird too—meetings run into lunch, someone brings food into the break room, then I’m thinking about whether I answered an email wrong or if I forgot to call somebody back… and then the whole day’s gone.\n\nSo, appetite specifically? I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, my clothes still basically fit, so I don’t think it’s been anything dramatic.\n\nBut I haven’t weighed myself. We have a scale somewhere—my wife moved it when she cleaned out the bathroom closet, and then that turned into this whole thing with old towels and boxes from when we renovated… sorry. I guess what I’m saying is I haven’t noticed a clear weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep pattern since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sleep’s been pretty bad, I guess. Most nights I’ll lie there and my brain just goes right back to the plant, like I’m replaying it whether I want to or not.\n\nAnd then if I do fall asleep, it’s not like good sleep. I’ll jolt awake from dreams about the line, or I’ll wake up at like 3 or 4 in the morning and just… feel like I didn’t sleep at all. Then I’m up thinking about stupid things too, like whether I left something on my desk or if I need to check the schedule, but really it always kind of circles back to that place.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself since the accident?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, “hurting myself” sounds very direct, and I don’t sit there making some kind of plan, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I get stuck in my head and everything feels like too much, and then I’ll think, you know, I just wish I could shut it off for a while. But then I start thinking about my wife needing the car serviced, or some thing at work that I’m supposed to handle, and it kind of turns into a whole list of stuff. So… I’m not sure. I haven’t really thought of it in those exact words.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life since the accident?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I don’t think of it like that exactly. “Ending my life” sounds so final, and I don’t sit down and, you know, make decisions about something like that.\n\nSometimes I just get tired of everything feeling so loud in my head, and then I end up thinking about completely other things, like bills or whether I replied to an email, or my son—well, he had this school thing last month and I almost missed it because I got stuck at work, and then I felt terrible about that too.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I guess I have a hard time separating what’s just wanting everything to stop for a minute from what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to die since the accident?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, wanting to die is a big way to put it, and I don’t usually say it to myself like that.\n\nSometimes I just feel worn out, like I don’t want to deal with another day of everything being like this, but then my mind jumps to something else, like whether I paid the mortgage or if someone at work is waiting on me for some report. And then I’m thinking about how the garage door’s been making that noise again, which sounds stupid, but it’s just… my head goes everywhere.\n\nSo I’m not sure how to answer that straight. I don’t know if I’d call it wanting to die.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of not wanting to live anymore?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I’d put it that way. Not wanting to live anymore sounds like… like I’ve reached some clear conclusion, and I don’t think that’s what happens in my head.\n\nIt’s more like I get overwhelmed and then I can’t tell if I mean I don’t want life, or I just don’t want the next phone call, the next errand, the next—like yesterday I spent twenty minutes looking for my keys and then realized they were in my coat pocket, and somehow that felt like proof I can’t keep anything straight. Which sounds ridiculous when I say it.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I don’t think I can give you a clean yes or no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had stretches where I felt really low, yeah, but “most of the day, nearly every day” is hard for me to pin down.\n\nSome days I wake up already feeling heavy, and then other days I get distracted by work stuff or something at home, and I’ll realize I haven’t really checked in with myself at all. Like last week I spent half the day dealing with some scheduling mess at work, and by the time I got home I couldn’t even tell if I was depressed or just exhausted.\n\nSo… I’m not saying no, exactly. I just don’t know how to measure it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt depressed for most of the day, for at least two weeks straight, at any point since the accident?\n\nPatient: I honestly don’t know. Two weeks straight feels… I mean, I lose track of days now. I can tell you there have been runs where everything felt kind of gray and heavy, but whether it was most of the day every day for two full weeks, I can’t say that cleanly.\n\nSometimes I’ll think, “Yeah, it’s been like this forever,” and then I remember there was a day where I got pulled into some work problem and didn’t notice it for a few hours. Or I had to go to the hardware store for something, and then I’m standing there comparing light bulbs like that matters, and suddenly I’m thinking about how the old bulbs in the garage flicker.\n\nSo… I don’t want to give you an answer that sounds more certain than I feel. I’ve had bad stretches since the accident, but I don’t know if they fit that exact two-week thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about wanting to die or ending your life since the accident?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know how to answer that exactly.\n\nSince the accident, I’ve definitely had moments where I just wanted everything to stop, but when you say “wanting to die” or “ending my life,” that feels very specific, and I don’t know that I’ve sat there and thought it through like that.\n\nSometimes it’s more like I’m driving home and I miss my exit because I’m thinking about something else, and then I get angry at myself for missing it, and then I’m just tired of being in my own head. But that’s not the same as making some kind of plan or… I don’t know.\n\nSo I guess I’m saying I’m not sure. I haven’t really sorted it into those words.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
